People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

I’m trying to turn my art into an actual freelance business, which is exciting, but I keep getting really wound up over things like invoices or client messages. Then I put them off, and it kind of snowballs until I’m crying at my kitchen table or forgetting normal things, like paying a bill. I know that probably sounds a little silly, but it’s been… more than I’m managing well on my own.

I know that probably sounds a little silly, but it’s been… more than I’m managing well on my own.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repetitive thoughts that you can't seem to get out of your head?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? Not in a way where it feels like a loop I can’t stop, if that makes sense. I mean, I do think about stressful things more than I’d like, but I can usually distract myself eventually. I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain tasks or situations because they make you feel anxious?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I do. Not big situations, exactly, but things like invoices or replying to client messages. I’ll tell myself I’m just waiting until I can do it properly, but really I’m getting anxious and putting it off. Then it builds up, and I end up really upset over it, which feels embarrassing to admit. I don’t know if that’s the kind of avoidance you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have to perform specific rituals or behaviors to reduce your anxiety?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not rituals, really. I mean, I might reread a message a few times before sending it, but I don’t have a specific thing I feel like I have to do to make the anxiety go away. At least, not that I’ve noticed. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring thoughts or images that are disturbing or intrusive to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like disturbing images popping into my head or anything. I mean, I worry about things, but it feels more like regular stress than something intrusive. I’m not sure if I’m explaining that right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about very specific things, like making mistakes or not meeting expectations?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think that’s probably a big part of it. It’s mostly with the freelance art stuff… like invoices or client messages. I get really worried I’m going to do something wrong or seem unprofessional, so I put it off.\n\nI know it sounds kind of small, but it can get to the point where I’m just sitting at the kitchen table crying over it, and then I’ll realize I’ve forgotten normal things too, like paying a bill. That’s actually why I came in, because it feels like I’m not handling it the way I should.\n\nDoctor: Is your worrying mostly about your freelance art business or does it also include other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Mostly the freelance art business, I think. That’s what kind of set it off, trying to make it a “real” thing and then getting stuck on invoices or client messages.\n\nIt does spill over a little, I guess… like when I avoid those things and get really wound up, I’ll end up crying at the kitchen table or forgetting normal stuff, like a bill. But the worry itself is mainly around the art work and doing it right, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Does the worrying about making mistakes or not meeting expectations extend beyond your freelance art business to other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mostly tied to the freelance art business. I don’t usually worry like this about everything, or at least not in the same way.\n\nIt’s just… ever since I tried to make the art into a real business, I get really wound up about invoices or client messages, like I’m going to mess something up or not handle it properly. Then I avoid it, and it kind of snowballs until I’m upset at the kitchen table or I’ve forgotten something normal, like paying a bill. I know that probably sounds silly, but that’s the part that made me feel like I should come in.\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety about your freelance art business affected your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so, not in a big way. I mean, I’m probably a little more withdrawn when I’m stressed about it, but I haven’t noticed it causing real problems with friends or family.\n\nI guess I might be quieter or not as quick to respond sometimes, but I don’t think anyone’s upset with me… at least I hope not. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi… thanks for seeing me. I guess I’m here because I’ve been having a harder time than I expected with stress. I’m trying to turn my art into an actual freelance business, which is exciting, but I keep getting really wound up over things like invoices or client messages. Then I put them off, and it kind of snowballs until I’m crying at my kitchen table or forgetting normal things, like paying a bill. I know that probably sounds a little silly, but it’s been… more than I’m managing well on my own.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repetitive thoughts that you can't seem to get out of your head?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? I mean, I worry about things, but it feels more like regular stress than something intrusive. I’m not sure if I’m explaining that right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about very specific things, like making mistakes or not meeting expectations?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think that’s probably a big part of it. I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain tasks or situations because they make you feel anxious?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I do. Not big situations, exactly, but things like invoices or replying to client messages. I’ll tell myself I’m just waiting until I can do it properly, but really I’m getting anxious and putting it off. Then it builds up, and I end up really upset over it, which feels embarrassing to admit. I don’t know if that’s the kind of avoidance you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have to perform specific rituals or behaviors to reduce your anxiety?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not rituals, really. I mean, I might reread a message a few times before sending it, but I don’t have a specific thing I feel like I have to do to make the anxiety go away. At least, not that I’ve noticed. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring thoughts or images that are disturbing or intrusive to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like disturbing images popping into my head or anything. I mean, I worry about things, but it feels more like regular stress than something intrusive. I’m not sure if I’m explaining that right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about very specific things, like making mistakes or not meeting expectations?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think that’s probably a big part of it. It’s mostly with the freelance art stuff… like invoices or client messages. I get really worried I’m going to do something wrong or seem unprofessional, so I put it off.\n\nI know it sounds kind of small, but it can get to the point where I’m just sitting at the kitchen table crying over it, and then I’ll realize I’ve forgotten normal things too, like paying a bill. That’s actually why I came in, because it feels like I’m not handling it the way I should.\n\nDoctor: Is your worrying mostly about your freelance art business or does it also include other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Mostly the freelance art business, I think. That’s what kind of set it off, trying to make it a “real” thing and then getting stuck on invoices or client messages.\n\nIt does spill over a little, I guess… like when I avoid those things and get really wound up, I’ll end up crying at the kitchen table or forgetting normal stuff, like a bill. But the worry itself is mainly around the art work and doing it right, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Does the worrying about making mistakes or not meeting expectations extend beyond your freelance art business to other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mostly tied to the freelance art business. I don’t usually worry like this about everything, or at least not in the same way.\n\nIt’s just… ever since I tried to make the art into a real business, I get really wound up about invoices or client messages, like I’m going to mess something up or not handle it properly. Then I avoid it, and it kind of snowballs until I’m upset at the kitchen table or I’ve forgotten something normal, like paying a bill. I know that probably sounds silly, but that’s the part that made me feel like I should come in.\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety about your freelance art business affected your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so, not in a big way. I mean, I’m probably a little more withdrawn when I’m stressed about it, but I haven’t noticed it causing real problems with friends or family.\n\nI guess I might be quieter or not as quick to respond sometimes, but I don’t think anyone’s upset with me… at least I hope not. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety about your freelance art business affected your ability to work or complete tasks on time?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it has started getting in the way.\n\nLike, I’ll put off replying to a client because I’m worried I’ll say the wrong thing, and then I’m behind. And recently I missed a utility payment while I was trying to keep up with the art display stuff. It’s not constant, but it’s enough that I’m kind of embarrassed by it.\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety about your freelance art business caused you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it has, though I feel a little embarrassed saying that. I came in because since I tried to make my art into an actual freelance business, I keep getting really wound up over things like invoices and client messages.\n\nAnd then instead of just doing them, I avoid them, and it builds up until I’m crying at the kitchen table or I forget normal things, like paying a bill. It’s not like I’m falling apart all the time, but it’s been upsetting enough that I thought maybe I should ask for help.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety only present when you're dealing with your freelance art business?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yes… I mean, it really seems tied to the freelance art stuff. Ever since I tried to make it into a real business, I get wound up over things like invoices or client messages, and then I avoid them.\n\nThen it kind of snowballs until I’m crying at the kitchen table or forgetting normal things, like paying a bill. But it’s not like I’m anxious about everything all the time, if that’s what you mean.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain tasks or situations because they make you feel anxious?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I do. Not big situations, exactly, but things like invoices or replying to client messages. I’ll tell myself I’m just waiting until I can do it properly, but really I’m getting anxious and putting it off. Then it builds up, and I end up really upset over it, which feels embarrassing to admit. I don’t know if that’s the kind of avoidance you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have to perform specific rituals or behaviors to reduce your anxiety?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not rituals, really. I mean, I might reread a message a few times before sending it, but I don’t have a specific thing I feel like I have to do to make the anxiety go away. At least, not that I’ve noticed. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring thoughts or images that are disturbing or intrusive to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like disturbing images popping into my head or anything. I mean, I worry about things, but it feels more like regular stress than something intrusive. I’m not sure if I’m explaining that right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about very specific things, like making mistakes or not meeting expectations?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think that’s probably a big part of it. It’s mostly with the freelance art stuff… like invoices or client messages. I get really worried I’m going to do something wrong or seem unprofessional, so I put it off.\n\nI know it sounds kind of small, but it can get to the point where I’m just sitting at the kitchen table crying over it, and then I’ll realize I’ve forgotten normal things too, like paying a bill. That’s actually why I came in, because it feels like I’m not handling it the way I should.\n\nDoctor: Is your worrying mostly about your freelance art business or does it also include other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Mostly the freelance art business, I think. That’s what kind of set it off, trying to make it a “real” thing and then getting stuck on invoices or client messages.\n\nIt does spill over a little, I guess… like when I avoid those things and get really wound up, I’ll end up crying at the kitchen table or forgetting normal stuff, like a bill. But the worry itself is mainly around the art work and doing it right, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Does the worrying about making mistakes or not meeting expectations extend beyond your freelance art business to other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mostly tied to the freelance art business. I don’t usually worry like this about everything, or at least not in the same way.\n\nIt’s just… ever since I tried to make the art into a real business, I get really wound up about invoices or client messages, like I’m going to mess something up or not handle it properly. Then I avoid it, and it kind of snowballs until I’m upset at the kitchen table or I’ve forgotten something normal, like paying a bill. I know that probably sounds silly, but that’s the part that made me feel like I should come in.\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety about your freelance art business affected your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so, not in a big way. I mean, I’m probably a little more withdrawn when I’m stressed about it, but I haven’t noticed it causing real problems with friends or family.\n\nI guess I might be quieter or not as quick to respond sometimes, but I don’t think anyone’s upset with me… at least I hope not. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety about your freelance art business affected your ability to work or complete tasks on time?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it has started getting in the way.\n\nLike, I’ll put off replying to a client because I’m worried I’ll say the wrong thing, and then I’m behind. And recently I missed a utility payment while I was trying to keep up with the art display stuff. It’s not constant, but it’s enough that I’m kind of embarrassed by it.\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety about your freelance art business caused you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it has, though I feel a little embarrassed saying that. I came in because since I tried to make my art into an actual freelance business, I keep getting really wound up over things like invoices and client messages.\n\nAnd then instead of just doing them, I avoid them, and it builds up until I’m crying at the kitchen table or I forget normal things, like paying a bill. It’s not like I’m falling apart all the time, but it’s been upsetting enough that I thought maybe I should ask for help.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety only present when you're dealing with your freelance art business?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yes… I mean, it really seems tied to the freelance art stuff. Ever since I tried to make it into a real business, I get wound up over things like invoices or client messages, and then I avoid them.\n\nThen it kind of snowballs until I’m crying at the kitchen table or forgetting normal things, like paying a bill. But it’s not like I’m anxious about everything all the time, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything else in your life, besides your freelance art business, that triggers this kind of anxiety for you?\n\nPatient: Not really… or at least, not in that same way.\n\nI mean, normal life stuff can stress me out a bit, sure, but this kind of spiraling seems to happen around the freelance art business. Since I tried to make it more “real,” the invoices and client messages get me really wound up, and then I avoid them, and then it turns into me crying at the kitchen table or forgetting something basic like paying a bill.\n\nSo… I think it’s mostly that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety about making mistakes or not meeting expectations limited to when you are working on your freelance art business?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think it’s pretty much limited to the freelance art business.\n\nI mean… I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but since I tried to turn my art into an actual business, I get really worried about messing up invoices or disappointing clients with messages. Then I avoid it, and it builds up until I’m crying at the kitchen table or I forget normal things, like paying a bill.\n\nSo yeah… it seems tied to that, if that answers the question.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

But the worry itself is mainly around the art work and doing it right, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Does the worrying about making mistakes or not meeting expectations extend beyond your freelance art business to other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mostly tied to the freelance art business. I don’t usually worry like this about everything, or at least not in the same way.\n\nIt’s just… ever since I tried to make the art into a real business, I get really wound up about invoices or client messages, like I’m going to mess something up or not handle it properly. Then I avoid it, and it kind of snowballs until I’m upset at the kitchen table or I’ve forgotten something normal, like paying a bill. I know that probably sounds silly, but that’s the part that made me feel like I should come in.\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety about your freelance art business affected your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so, not in a big way. I mean, I’m probably a little more withdrawn when I’m stressed about it, but I haven’t noticed it causing real problems with friends or family.\n\nI guess I might be quieter or not as quick to respond sometimes, but I don’t think anyone’s upset with me… at least I hope not. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety about your freelance art business affected your ability to work or complete tasks on time?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it has started getting in the way.\n\nLike, I’ll put off replying to a client because I’m worried I’ll say the wrong thing, and then I’m behind. And recently I missed a utility payment while I was trying to keep up with the art display stuff. It’s not constant, but it’s enough that I’m kind of embarrassed by it.\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety about your freelance art business caused you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it has, though I feel a little embarrassed saying that. I came in because since I tried to make my art into an actual freelance business, I keep getting really wound up over things like invoices and client messages.\n\nAnd then instead of just doing them, I avoid them, and it builds up until I’m crying at the kitchen table or I forget normal things, like paying a bill. It’s not like I’m falling apart all the time, but it’s been upsetting enough that I thought maybe I should ask for help.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety only present when you're dealing with your freelance art business?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yes… I mean, it really seems tied to the freelance art stuff. Ever since I tried to make it into a real business, I get wound up over things like invoices or client messages, and then I avoid them.\n\nThen it kind of snowballs until I’m crying at the kitchen table or forgetting normal things, like paying a bill. But it’s not like I’m anxious about everything all the time, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything else in your life, besides your freelance art business, that triggers this kind of anxiety for you?\n\nPatient: Not really… or at least, not in that same way.\n\nI mean, normal life stuff can stress me out a bit, sure, but this kind of spiraling seems to happen around the freelance art business. Since I tried to make it more “real,” the invoices and client messages get me really wound up, and then I avoid them, and then it turns into me crying at the kitchen table or forgetting something basic like paying a bill.\n\nSo… I think it’s mostly that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety about making mistakes or not meeting expectations limited to when you are working on your freelance art business?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think it’s pretty much limited to the freelance art business.\n\nI mean… I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but since I tried to turn my art into an actual business, I get really worried about messing up invoices or disappointing clients with messages. Then I avoid it, and it builds up until I’m crying at the kitchen table or I forget normal things, like paying a bill.\n\nSo yeah… it seems tied to that, if that answers the question.\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety about your freelance art business made it difficult for you to meet your responsibilities or achieve your goals?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it has, though I don’t want to make it sound like everything is falling apart.\n\nIt’s started getting in the way a bit. Like, I put off replying to a client because I was so anxious about saying the wrong thing, and then I missed a utility payment while I was trying to keep up with an art display. That was embarrassing.\n\nSo… yes, I guess it is affecting both the freelance work and some home responsibilities. Does that count as what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Then I avoid it, and it kind of snowballs until I’m upset at the kitchen table or I’ve forgotten something normal, like paying a bill. I know that probably sounds silly, but that’s the part that made me feel like I should come in.\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety about your freelance art business affected your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so, not in a big way. I mean, I’m probably a little more withdrawn when I’m stressed about it, but I haven’t noticed it causing real problems with friends or family.\n\nI guess I might be quieter or not as quick to respond sometimes, but I don’t think anyone’s upset with me… at least I hope not. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety about your freelance art business affected your ability to work or complete tasks on time?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it has started getting in the way.\n\nLike, I’ll put off replying to a client because I’m worried I’ll say the wrong thing, and then I’m behind. And recently I missed a utility payment while I was trying to keep up with the art display stuff. It’s not constant, but it’s enough that I’m kind of embarrassed by it.\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety about your freelance art business caused you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it has, though I feel a little embarrassed saying that. I came in because since I tried to make my art into an actual freelance business, I keep getting really wound up over things like invoices and client messages.\n\nAnd then instead of just doing them, I avoid them, and it builds up until I’m crying at the kitchen table or I forget normal things, like paying a bill. It’s not like I’m falling apart all the time, but it’s been upsetting enough that I thought maybe I should ask for help.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety only present when you're dealing with your freelance art business?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yes… I mean, it really seems tied to the freelance art stuff. Ever since I tried to make it into a real business, I get wound up over things like invoices or client messages, and then I avoid them.\n\nThen it kind of snowballs until I’m crying at the kitchen table or forgetting normal things, like paying a bill. But it’s not like I’m anxious about everything all the time, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything else in your life, besides your freelance art business, that triggers this kind of anxiety for you?\n\nPatient: Not really… or at least, not in that same way.\n\nI mean, normal life stuff can stress me out a bit, sure, but this kind of spiraling seems to happen around the freelance art business. Since I tried to make it more “real,” the invoices and client messages get me really wound up, and then I avoid them, and then it turns into me crying at the kitchen table or forgetting something basic like paying a bill.\n\nSo… I think it’s mostly that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety about making mistakes or not meeting expectations limited to when you are working on your freelance art business?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think it’s pretty much limited to the freelance art business.\n\nI mean… I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but since I tried to turn my art into an actual business, I get really worried about messing up invoices or disappointing clients with messages. Then I avoid it, and it builds up until I’m crying at the kitchen table or I forget normal things, like paying a bill.\n\nSo yeah… it seems tied to that, if that answers the question.\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety about your freelance art business made it difficult for you to meet your responsibilities or achieve your goals?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it has, though I don’t want to make it sound like everything is falling apart.\n\nIt’s started getting in the way a bit. Like, I put off replying to a client because I was so anxious about saying the wrong thing, and then I missed a utility payment while I was trying to keep up with an art display. That was embarrassing.\n\nSo… yes, I guess it is affecting both the freelance work and some home responsibilities. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety about your freelance art business focused on a specific part of it, like the creative work itself or more on the business side of things?\n\nPatient: It’s more the business side, I think. The art itself is usually okay… it’s when it starts feeling official, like invoices or client messages, that I get really wound up.\n\nAnd then I kind of avoid it, which I know doesn’t help, and it can build until I’m crying at the kitchen table or even forgetting normal things like paying a bill. I’m not sure if I’m explaining that clearly, but yes—more the business parts than the creative work.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. But the worry itself is mainly around the art work and doing it right, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Does the worrying about making mistakes or not meeting expectations extend beyond your freelance art business to other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mostly tied to the freelance art business. I don’t usually worry like this about everything, or at least not in the same way.\n\nIt’s just… ever since I tried to make the art into a real business, I get really wound up about invoices or client messages, like I’m going to mess something up or not handle it properly. Then I avoid it, and it kind of snowballs until I’m upset at the kitchen table or I’ve forgotten something normal, like paying a bill. I know that probably sounds silly, but that’s the part that made me feel like I should come in.\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety about your freelance art business affected your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so, not in a big way. I mean, I’m probably a little more withdrawn when I’m stressed about it, but I haven’t noticed it causing real problems with friends or family.\n\nI guess I might be quieter or not as quick to respond sometimes, but I don’t think anyone’s upset with me… at least I hope not. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety about your freelance art business affected your ability to work or complete tasks on time?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it has started getting in the way.\n\nLike, I’ll put off replying to a client because I’m worried I’ll say the wrong thing, and then I’m behind. And recently I missed a utility payment while I was trying to keep up with the art display stuff. It’s not constant, but it’s enough that I’m kind of embarrassed by it.\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety about your freelance art business caused you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it has, though I feel a little embarrassed saying that. I came in because since I tried to make my art into an actual freelance business, I keep getting really wound up over things like invoices and client messages.\n\nAnd then instead of just doing them, I avoid them, and it builds up until I’m crying at the kitchen table or I forget normal things, like paying a bill. It’s not like I’m falling apart all the time, but it’s been upsetting enough that I thought maybe I should ask for help.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety only present when you're dealing with your freelance art business?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yes… I mean, it really seems tied to the freelance art stuff. Ever since I tried to make it into a real business, I get wound up over things like invoices or client messages, and then I avoid them.\n\nThen it kind of snowballs until I’m crying at the kitchen table or forgetting normal things, like paying a bill. But it’s not like I’m anxious about everything all the time, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything else in your life, besides your freelance art business, that triggers this kind of anxiety for you?\n\nPatient: Not really… or at least, not in that same way.\n\nI mean, normal life stuff can stress me out a bit, sure, but this kind of spiraling seems to happen around the freelance art business. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety about making mistakes or not meeting expectations limited to when you are working on your freelance art business?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think it’s pretty much limited to the freelance art business.\n\nI mean… I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but since I tried to turn my art into an actual business, I get really worried about messing up invoices or disappointing clients with messages. Like, I put off replying to a client because I was so anxious about saying the wrong thing, and then I missed a utility payment while I was trying to keep up with an art display. That was embarrassing.\n\nSo… yes, I guess it is affecting both the freelance work and some home responsibilities. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety about your freelance art business focused on a specific part of it, like the creative work itself or more on the business side of things?\n\nPatient: It’s more the business side, I think. I don’t know if that’s the kind of avoidance you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have to perform specific rituals or behaviors to reduce your anxiety?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not rituals, really. I mean, I might reread a message a few times before sending it, but I don’t have a specific thing I feel like I have to do to make the anxiety go away. At least, not that I’ve noticed. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring thoughts or images that are disturbing or intrusive to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like disturbing images popping into my head or anything. I mean, I worry about things, but it feels more like regular stress than something intrusive. I’m not sure if I’m explaining that right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about very specific things, like making mistakes or not meeting expectations?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think that’s probably a big part of it. It’s mostly with the freelance art stuff… like invoices or client messages. I get really worried I’m going to do something wrong or seem unprofessional, so I put it off.\n\nI know it sounds kind of small, but it can get to the point where I’m just sitting at the kitchen table crying over it, and then I’ll realize I’ve forgotten normal things too, like paying a bill. That’s actually why I came in, because it feels like I’m not handling it the way I should.\n\nDoctor: Is your worrying mostly about your freelance art business or does it also include other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Mostly the freelance art business, I think. That’s what kind of set it off, trying to make it a “real” thing and then getting stuck on invoices or client messages.\n\nIt does spill over a little, I guess… like when I avoid those things and get really wound up, I’ll end up crying at the kitchen table or forgetting normal stuff, like a bill. But the worry itself is mainly around the art work and doing it right, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Does the worrying about making mistakes or not meeting expectations extend beyond your freelance art business to other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mostly tied to the freelance art business. I don’t usually worry like this about everything, or at least not in the same way.\n\nIt’s just… ever since I tried to make the art into a real business, I get really wound up about invoices or client messages, like I’m going to mess something up or not handle it properly. Then I avoid it, and it kind of snowballs until I’m upset at the kitchen table or I’ve forgotten something normal, like paying a bill. I know that probably sounds silly, but that’s the part that made me feel like I should come in.\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety about your freelance art business affected your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so, not in a big way. I mean, I’m probably a little more withdrawn when I’m stressed about it, but I haven’t noticed it causing real problems with friends or family.\n\nI guess I might be quieter or not as quick to respond sometimes, but I don’t think anyone’s upset with me… at least I hope not. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety about your freelance art business affected your ability to work or complete tasks on time?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it has started getting in the way.\n\nLike, I’ll put off replying to a client because I’m worried I’ll say the wrong thing, and then I’m behind. And recently I missed a utility payment while I was trying to keep up with the art display stuff. It’s not constant, but it’s enough that I’m kind of embarrassed by it.\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety about your freelance art business caused you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it has, though I feel a little embarrassed saying that. I came in because since I tried to make my art into an actual freelance business, I keep getting really wound up over things like invoices and client messages.\n\nAnd then instead of just doing them, I avoid them, and it builds up until I’m crying at the kitchen table or I forget normal things, like paying a bill. It’s not like I’m falling apart all the time, but it’s been upsetting enough that I thought maybe I should ask for help.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety only present when you're dealing with your freelance art business?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yes… I mean, it really seems tied to the freelance art stuff. Ever since I tried to make it into a real business, I get wound up over things like invoices or client messages, and then I avoid them.\n\nThen it kind of snowballs until I’m crying at the kitchen table or forgetting normal things, like paying a bill. But it’s not like I’m anxious about everything all the time, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything else in your life, besides your freelance art business, that triggers this kind of anxiety for you?\n\nPatient: Not really… or at least, not in that same way.\n\nI mean, normal life stuff can stress me out a bit, sure, but this kind of spiraling seems to happen around the freelance art business. Since I tried to make it more “real,” the invoices and client messages get me really wound up, and then I avoid them, and then it turns into me crying at the kitchen table or forgetting something basic like paying a bill.\n\nSo… I think it’s mostly that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety about making mistakes or not meeting expectations limited to when you are working on your freelance art business?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think it’s pretty much limited to the freelance art business.\n\nI mean… I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but since I tried to turn my art into an actual business, I get really worried about messing up invoices or disappointing clients with messages. Then I avoid it, and it builds up until I’m crying at the kitchen table or I forget normal things, like paying a bill.\n\nSo yeah… it seems tied to that, if that answers the question.\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety about your freelance art business made it difficult for you to meet your responsibilities or achieve your goals?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it has, though I don’t want to make it sound like everything is falling apart.\n\nIt’s started getting in the way a bit. Like, I put off replying to a client because I was so anxious about saying the wrong thing, and then I missed a utility payment while I was trying to keep up with an art display. That was embarrassing.\n\nSo… yes, I guess it is affecting both the freelance work and some home responsibilities. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety about your freelance art business focused on a specific part of it, like the creative work itself or more on the business side of things?\n\nPatient: It’s more the business side, I think. The art itself is usually okay… it’s when it starts feeling official, like invoices or client messages, that I get really wound up.\n\nAnd then I kind of avoid it, which I know doesn’t help, and it can build until I’m crying at the kitchen table or even forgetting normal things like paying a bill. I’m not sure if I’m explaining that clearly, but yes—more the business parts than the creative work.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still worry about invoices and client messages if you weren't doing freelance art?\n\nPatient: Probably not… or at least not in the same way.\n\nI mean, invoices and client messages only really became a thing once I tried to make my art into a real freelance business. That’s when I started getting so wound up about them, and then I’d put them off until it felt bigger than it was. Sometimes I’d end up crying at the kitchen table or forgetting normal things, like paying a bill.\n\nSo I think it’s tied to the freelance art situation, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about making mistakes or not meeting expectations in other areas of your life, outside of your freelance art business?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, no. I mean, I don’t love making mistakes anywhere, obviously, but this feels pretty tied to the freelance art business.\n\nThat’s really why I came in—since I tried to make the art into an actual business, I get so wound up over invoices and client messages, and then I avoid them. Then it turns into me crying at the kitchen table or forgetting normal things, like paying a bill.\n\nSo… outside of that, I don’t think it’s been the same kind of worry. Is that the sort of distinction you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety about making mistakes or not meeting expectations limited to when you are working on your freelance art business?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think it’s pretty much limited to the freelance art business.\n\nI mean… I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but since I tried to turn my art into an actual business, I get really worried about messing up invoices or disappointing clients with messages. Then I avoid it, and it builds up until I’m crying at the kitchen table or I forget normal things, like paying a bill.\n\nSo yeah… it seems tied to that, if that answers the question.\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety about your freelance art business made it difficult for you to meet your responsibilities or achieve your goals?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it has, though I don’t want to make it sound like everything is falling apart.\n\nIt’s started getting in the way a bit. Like, I put off replying to a client because I was so anxious about saying the wrong thing, and then I missed a utility payment while I was trying to keep up with an art display. That was embarrassing.\n\nSo… yes, I guess it is affecting both the freelance work and some home responsibilities. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety about your freelance art business focused on a specific part of it, like the creative work itself or more on the business side of things?\n\nPatient: It’s more the business side, I think. The art itself is usually okay… it’s when it starts feeling official, like invoices or client messages, that I get really wound up.\n\nAnd then I kind of avoid it, which I know doesn’t help, and it can build until I’m crying at the kitchen table or even forgetting normal things like paying a bill. I’m not sure if I’m explaining that clearly, but yes—more the business parts than the creative work.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still worry about invoices and client messages if you weren't doing freelance art?\n\nPatient: Probably not… or at least not in the same way.\n\nI mean, invoices and client messages only really became a thing once I tried to make my art into a real freelance business. That’s when I started getting so wound up about them, and then I’d put them off until it felt bigger than it was. Sometimes I’d end up crying at the kitchen table or forgetting normal things, like paying a bill.\n\nSo I think it’s tied to the freelance art situation, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about making mistakes or not meeting expectations in other areas of your life, outside of your freelance art business?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, no. I mean, I don’t love making mistakes anywhere, obviously, but this feels pretty tied to the freelance art business.\n\nThat’s really why I came in—since I tried to make the art into an actual business, I get so wound up over invoices and client messages, and then I avoid them. Then it turns into me crying at the kitchen table or forgetting normal things, like paying a bill.\n\nSo… outside of that, I don’t think it’s been the same kind of worry. Is that the sort of distinction you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worrying about invoices and client messages would stop if you were to stop doing freelance art?\n\nPatient: I think it might, yes… but I’m not completely sure, because I haven’t really had a clean break from it.\n\nThe contract stuff and the business changes are still kind of happening, so there hasn’t been a stretch where it’s all been over for months and I could see if the worrying actually settled down. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: If you were to stop doing freelance art, do you think your overall level of anxiety would significantly decrease?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think it probably would decrease a lot… or at least the part that feels the most overwhelming would.\n\nI don’t want to overstate it, but this really started after I tried to make my art into a proper freelance business. The invoices and client messages are what I get so wound up about, and then I avoid them until it turns into me crying at the kitchen table or forgetting normal things, like paying a bill.\n\nSo if I stopped doing freelance art, I’d imagine a lot of that anxiety would ease up.
